## 4. Phases in detail

| Phase | Ships | Done when |
|---|---|---|
| **0 · Foundation** | WS01 → the public-answer trim → WS02 → WS03 (strip, live and recap blocks flagged off). WS01 is built and merged (Thu, Oct 22, 2026) | axe is clean on key routes; no Level-A failures; new nav live; homepage rebuilt with the hero ladder; preview DB in place; `newLook` and `themeHeritage` turned on once K has made the checks WS01 left him ("K's checks before the switches go on" in `REDESIGN_STATUS.md`), and the slideshow deleted after that (WS03); `clubEmailHidden` and `rosterShortNames` on once K has looked at the trim on a preview (Q14, Q38) |
| **1 · Events core** | Tournament creation, now (D20 to D22, D24): WS08's creation roles first, behind `standingDirectors`. Then WS04, WS05 (with festivals), WS06, plus WS13's member and parent sections (the one page, `newDashboard`) | A family can find an event by filter, map or calendar, read a plain-language event page, and register three players in one payment with one receipt; partner pages and share previews work; a festival page can go live; a standing director creates an event that is listed the moment it is published, and the LCA admins are told |
| **2 · Event day** | WS07's migration (tables and columns only), then WS08 (console, setup checklist, QR check-in), then WS07's pages | **Pilot at one LCA event** (for example the Paul Morphy Open) with alerts (`roundAlerts`) limited to registered players who have not switched off and their guardians, and followers left out (`followerAlerts` off); after the pilot review alerts run site-wide and K turns `followerAlerts` on. Before the pilot, a test upload of the rating report from a finished event has been accepted by US Chess and recorded in the console (assumed, P2.10) |
| **3 · Community** | WS09, then WS10, then WS11. Tournament creation, later (D20, D21): the regional representatives create their region's club events and name one-event directors from My region (assumed, Q1), each event listing once an LCA admin approves it (assumed, Q15) | Every club has its listing and page from `Clubs-H` and `Club-G`, with its meeting statement in its own words or "Schedule not listed", and each representative gets the monthly confirm email; a representative creates a club event and names its director; first results archived; recaps written in the Recap tab; the Scholastic page live with the season block. The this-week strip waits for P2.14 |
| **4 · Membership, governance, admin** | WS12, WS14, WS08's admin home and queues, WS13's remaining sections (the rail, `myLcaRail`). Tournament creation, last (D25 to D27): member-created events, behind the `member_events_enabled` admin setting | Join/renew/donate/card live; the tabbed About page with the bylaws reader live; one admin queue; once an LCA admin turns the setting on, an active member creates an event that no list shows, and its page, checkout, emails and printed kit say it is not an LCA event |
| **5 · Extras** | WS15, Wallet passes (D4), Web Push, Grand Prix series, hall TV polish, team and coach entries (assumed, P2.26) | as each item's acceptance criteria say |
| **Last · Walkthrough** | A walkthrough of everything built, for the LCA board (D30; the phase 2 replan's section 8 reads it as every page and every role's screens, with sample data, from one place) | planned and built only once the last workstream has landed |

Sequencing notes:
- **The public-answer trim** (WS01 follow-up "Public responses expose personal and internal fields"; D29), a small PR of its own before WS02: `GET /api/clubs/[id]` stops answering each officer's email, and `GET /api/tournaments` and `GET /api/tournaments/[id]` stop answering `keep_apart`, `created_by` and `report_settings` to anyone who does not manage the event. Each endpoint takes its contract in `domain/contracts` and a contract test with a role-safety case, and leaves `test/unit/contracts-pending.ts`. No page shows these fields (today's club page lists its officers by name and role only, and the manage page reads its own endpoint), so nothing anyone sees changes and no switch is needed (0.2). The same PR adds two switches, off, for the two things it changes that today's pages show, each with its server constant (`CLUB_EMAIL_HIDDEN` in `functions/utils/clubs/publicAnswer.ts`, `ROSTER_SHORT_NAMES` in `functions/utils/events/publicAnswer.ts`; `test/unit/public-answer-switches.test.ts` holds each equal to its switch, and `test/integration/public-answer-trim.test.ts` reads `GET /api/clubs/[id]` and `GET /api/tournaments/[id]` with each constant set and unset, as a manager and as anyone else), which K turns on after the preview: `clubEmailHidden` (`CLUB_EMAIL_HIDDEN`) takes the club's own `contact_email` off today's club page and out of `GET /api/clubs/[id]`, with "Contact this club through LCA" in its place (assumed, Q14); and `rosterShortNames` (`ROSTER_SHORT_NAMES`) writes under-18 entrants as first name and last initial in the `roster` of `GET /api/tournaments/[id]` for anyone who does not manage the event, through `publicName(..., 'entrants')`, while `computeStandings` keeps reading full names, so standings and prizes keep them (D3; assumed, Q38). Under 18 means what WS04's entrants rule reads: an active guardian link (`members.guardian_id`) or an entry with a confirmed scholastic range (`registrations.grade`).
- WS03's blocks light up as WS05 (the Featured rung), WS07 and WS10 land, each behind a flag; WS09 gives the this-week strip no data, so it waits for P2.14.
- WS05 and WS06 lean on each other: WS05's page lands first with today's registration in its card, and WS06 then swaps in its sheet and the household checkout. WS05's during state and its round alerts card fill in once WS07 lands, behind `roundAlerts`.
- **The household optimizer.** WS06's membership step (Phase 1) prices the household with the optimizer's rule, so WS06 builds that rule beside `domain/membership/tiers.ts`, with WS12's unit tests for it, and WS12's page (Phase 4) puts its two questions in front of the same rule.
- **WS07's migration first.** Phase 2 starts with WS07's `00xx_live_mode.sql` as a PR of its own that adds only tables and columns, with the fill of `tournament_games.section_id`, since WS08's console and setup write them: `round_publications`, `live_state` and `current_round` (Publish round), `tv_layout` and `tv_show_standings` (Hall TV), `tournament_sections.room_note` (the Sections step), the result confirmation columns and `result_reports` (Confirm), and `rate_limits`. Then WS08's console and setup checklist, with the publish action WS07 needs; then WS07's pages behind `newLive`, `resultReports` and `roundAlerts`.
- QR check-in (Phase 2) reads the signed token on WS12's card; `functions/utils/tokens.ts` comes earlier, in WS06, so the console's check-in is built in Phase 2 and scans cards once WS12 issues them in Phase 4.
- **My LCA.** WS13's one page (`newDashboard`) ships in Phase 1 with WS06; its event-day views fill in as WS07 lands, and its rail (`myLcaRail`) comes in Phase 4, each entry once its room's switch is on (`tdConsole`, `newClubs`, `adminHome`). WS02's My LCA menu keeps today's role rows until `myLcaRail` is on.
- **Public forms in Phase 3.** WS09 builds the spam helper, reuses WS07's rate-limit helper (Phase 2), and adds the region target to `POST /api/contact`, with its contract; the whole-board target comes with whichever of WS10 and WS11 lands first; WS14 reuses both in Phase 4.
- WS04's Season at a glance strip is built with WS11 in Phase 3, behind `seasonSchedule`; WS04's Phase 1 PRs do not wait for it, and WS04's criterion 15 is checked when WS11 lands (assumed, P2.22).
- **Tournament creation, now** (D20 to D22, D24). K wrote "Right now", so WS08's creation roles come first in Phase 1, on today's setup and manage pages, and do not wait for the Phase 2 TD console (assumed, Q11): the standing director role (Q4), the president's tournaments and one-event directors, admins naming one-event directors, a standing director's event listed the moment it is published, and the entry-fee rule with its plain notice. The same work carries WS08's `00xx_ticket_kinds.sql` (the ticket kinds WS04's `listing_report` uses, and `review_notified_at`), which merges before WS04. While `ADMIN_HOME_ON` is unset, the notification about a standing director's published event reaches the LCA admins by email, once per event (`review_notified_at`); once it is set, with `adminHome` in Phase 4, it lands in the queue.
- **Tournament creation, later** (D20, D21). The regional representatives create their region's club events, and name one-event directors, from WS09's My region in Phase 3 (assumed, Q1), with `CLUB_PAGES_ON`; each such event lists only after an LCA admin approves it (assumed, Q15). The entry-fee rule applies to them: representatives are not on D24's list.
- **Tournament creation, last** (D25 to D27). Member-created events ship in Phase 4 with WS08's admin home, since their switch lives in Admin › Site and "Report this event" lands in the admin queue; the setting stays off until K has tested. Each workstream they touch builds its part in that Phase 4 work rather than inside its earlier PRs: WS08 (creation with the organizer's tick, the limit of three open events, Report this event, the admin view of every member-created event and its use, closing an event, removing a member's ability to create, the rating report card that refuses LCA's IDs, `markMemberEventEmail`, which every sender of an email about an event calls, and the console's announcement email through it), WS05 (the page for anyone with the link, "Organized by <name>. Not an LCA event." and noindex), WS06 (the checkout notice, the organizer as the emails' reply-to, and the entry-fee rule), WS04 (out of every list, calendar feed, map and structured data, and the D27 line and the organizer's reply-to on its reminder emails, sent by `workers/daily-emails` and `registrationOpenNotify.ts`), WS09 (out of the sitemap it writes, through `listedWhere`), WS10 (out of the monthly results, the champions archive and the digest), WS03 (out of the home page), and WS07 (the same line on its live pages, printed kit and round emails, Q12). WS15, which comes later, builds them in from the start: the scanner on them, with the same allowance, and its emails through `markMemberEventEmail`. The public lists built on WS03's shared event query (section 2.2) take the exclusion from it, while a person's own events still show them.
- **The JSON columns.** WS05's `00xx_event_page.sql` drops `sections` and `round_schedule`, with the four 0053 triggers, whatever an earlier migration adds to `tournaments` first (WS04's slug, WS08's Phase 1 column); if something still reads a JSON column then, the drop moves to WS08's next migration that changes `tournaments`, `00xx_console.sql` in Phase 2 (`REDESIGN_STATUS.md`, WS01 Follow-ups).
- **The rating report fix.** The US Chess 2C fix to today's export (`DESIGN_REPLAN_phase2.md` section 3.8 and `docs/redesign/uschess-fix-brief.md`) lands ahead of WS08's Rating report step, which builds on the fixed writer; it follows Q8's defaults until K answers. Rating reports serve every event (D23), member-created ones included.
- **The walkthrough** (D30) comes after everything else and is the very last item: it is not planned or built until the last workstream has landed.

## 5. Decisions (settled by K, Thursday, October 8 to Wednesday, October 21, 2026)

| ID | Decision | Settled |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | Logo and mark | **Both, chosen by place (amended Thursday, October 8, 2026).** The rook-shaped Louisiana mark is the header's brand mark at every width (desktop, tablet and phone, with no cream plate and no swap on scroll), and also goes on the favicon, small badges, QR centres and social avatars. The board-voted full-colour LCA logo goes to the footer brand block, homepage identity band, About and history pages, emails, print kit, certificates and the membership card. Never recolour the logo; set it on white or cream. |
| D2 | Card processing fee | **All-in pricing (default).** The price shown is the price paid, with no separate card-fee line. LCA absorbs Stripe's fee of roughly 2.9% + 30¢ per payment. If that matters, nudge entry fees up instead of adding a line. K confirms with the board. |
| D3 | Names of minors | **Full names.** Results, standings, winners and recaps congratulate kids by name. The rest of the original decision, a page that lists minors and nothing else abbreviating them through a per-event `public_minor_names` setting, is replaced by D15. |
| D4 | Wallet passes | **Phase 5.** Start with the in-account card, the printable card and the QR. |
| D5 | Financial need | **LCA doesn't get involved.** No fee waivers and no lunch-status or need questions anywhere. Remove the lunch-discount line shown on the Event-A and Schol-A boards. |
| D6 | Who enters results | **The event's TDs, plus LCA admins** on any event as a backstop, enter and confirm every result. **Players report their own game as a steer** (amended from K's note of Fri, Oct 9, 2026: the TD sees a report "even when both players have not gotten done reporting it", and a report "should not determine the match result"; in the first build, assumed, P2.2): one report reaches the TD at once, with no opponent confirmation; a report never sets a result; the TD confirms it in one tap or enters something else, and the TD's entry is final. |
| D7 | How live pages update | **Polling.** Each open page checks for changes about every 20 seconds. It's simple, free, and plenty for 100 players. Use a push connection (Durable Objects) only if ever needed. |
| D8 | Text-message alerts | **Not now.** Email and web push are free. Texts cost money per message and need US carrier registration; revisit if LCA decides to pay for them. |
| D9 | Player pages | **None.** LCA is community-focused, not player-focused, so results stay on event pages, recaps and the champions page. |
| D10 | Club regions | **Assigned** in Appendix C: applied locally with `club_regions.sql`, and in production by WS09's numbered migration, `00xx_club_regions.sql`, which fills only a blank region. K corrects any that are off. |
| D11 | One entry price | **No member price and no member discount anywhere** (settled by K, Thursday, October 8, 2026). `member_discount` is retired from pricing; a section has one fee per tier, and only the tiers the setup has (Early, Regular, Late, with Regular required). Every board and the WS04, WS05, WS06, WS08 and WS12 text follow. |
| D12 | LCA membership to enter | **Required for LCA-run events** (settled by K, Thursday, October 8, 2026). The checkout adds the right membership for anyone in the order without an active one, in the same payment: the Family plan covers the guardian and up to 3 children, a 4th child is added with a Scholastic membership ($5), an adult joins at the Adult, Senior or Family tier. The per-tournament `requires_lca_membership` (default on) lets a club running its own event switch it off; a site-wide override, `membership_required_for_all` (admin only, default off), is planned so the board can force it on every event later without a code change. |
| D13 | One player, one profile | **Children are player profiles linked to guardians, not accounts** (settled by K, Thursday, October 8, 2026). A profile is a `members` row for life, linked through `member_guardians`; it needs no email; a login is optional from 13 with the guardian's recorded consent; no birthdate, grade or age is stored; a second guardian is in scope now. |
| D14 | Receipts | **One receipt system for every payment** (settled by K, Thursday, October 8, 2026). Every completed payment sends one receipt to the payer and one confirmation per player; Stripe's own receipts are turned off; `stripe_events` makes the webhook idempotent before any email is sent, so nobody gets two. |
| D15 | Names on pre-event lists | **First name and last initial for under-18s on pre-event entrants lists** (settled by K, Thursday, October 8, 2026): a player with an active guardian link, or an entry with a ticked eligibility box, shows as "Priya S." on See who's registered and Who's coming; no club is shown beside a player; TDs and admins see full names; results, standings, winners and recaps keep full names, as D3 says. This replaces the per-event `public_minor_names` mechanism and the "minors-only page" trigger in D3 and in 0.1 rule 3, which reads this way since 1.4. |
| D16 | Long forms and pages | **Scroll in their own container with sticky chrome** (settled by K, Thursday, October 8, 2026: "same with any other page"). The setup form scrolls inside a container with the steps rail and the page preview sticky beside it; phone sheets and the family picker scroll inside the sheet; a sticky bar never covers a focused control (`scroll-margin`, `--bottom-chrome-h`). |
| D17 | Who keeps club pages and listings | **The club's own rep, with the region's representative and LCA admins** (settled by K, Fri, Oct 9 and Thu, Oct 15, 2026). On Fri, Oct 9 K wrote "only regional reps will have access to editing their clubs now"; on Thu, Oct 15 K picked `Club-G`, whose page the club's own rep keeps, and asked to plan for its blocks to "go unused for a while until the club reps start inputting stuff". So a club keeps a named rep who keeps its page and listing; the representative whose board seat covers the club's region can edit that page too, and LCA admins can edit any club page. A request to be named a club's rep is approved by the region's representative, with LCA admins as the backstop (K took the default on Mon, Oct 19, 2026). Players are normally not associated with clubs ("normally players are not associated with a club"): no public roster, leaderboard or member count. The `club_rep` role stays; the rest of P2.18 is still open. |
| D18 | News and Facebook | **Facebook first, highlights not results** (settled by K, Fri, Oct 9, 2026). Most posting happens on the LCA Facebook page, so News is built to work with it: the site mirrors those posts into News with a link back (the way the phase 2 replan meets K's note; assumed, P2.42), and admins can promote one to a story (admins only, assumed, P2.32). News carries tournament highlights that link to each event's results page, never a results page of its own. |
| D19 | US Chess rating report | **Imports cleanly, tested first** (settled by K, Fri, Oct 9, 2026). The export must import into US Chess's rating report system without hand edits, and K test-uploads it before any real event. The phase 2 replan adds an automated check against US Chess's file layout and holds the Phase 2 pilot until a test upload imports cleanly (assumed, P2.10). |
| D20 | Who may create a tournament | **In three phases** (settled by K, Mon, Oct 19, 2026). **Now:** a standing director role for the community's trusted tournament directors, "to always be able to make their tournaments here for the purpose of testing things out and spreading its usage"; the president, who "should be able to make tournaments always"; and the LCA admins, who can already create them. **Later:** the regional representatives, for their region's clubs (from WS09's My region in Phase 3, assumed, Q1). **Last:** every active LCA member, never listed (D25 to D27). How a standing director is named is Q4; whether a club rep also creates the club's events is Q2. |
| D21 | Who drafts a tournament and names a one-event director | **The president, the LCA admins and the regional representatives** (settled by K, Mon, Oct 19, 2026: "president, admins, and regions reps"); no other officer. A one-event director is named on that one event through `tournament_directors` and runs it from the TD console. The representatives do this from the phase in which they start creating (D20, Q1). |
| D22 | When a standing director's event is listed | **The moment it is published, with nothing ever held** (settled by K, Mon, Oct 19, 2026: "as soon as they publish, but send a notification to the LCA Admin to review after the fact just in case, but no holds"). A notification goes to the LCA admins to review the event after the fact. |
| D23 | US Chess rating reports | **For every event, listed or not** (settled by K, Mon, Oct 19, 2026: "Rating reports available for everyone, i see no reason to restrict this, convince me if otherwise"). LCA's affiliate ID goes only on listed events and on events run by standing directors, the president, admins and representatives (K took the default on Mon, Oct 19, 2026); a member-created event's report carries only the organizer's own affiliate and director IDs (D26), so the one reason found to restrict reports, US Chess billing LCA, does not arise. |
| D24 | Entry fees above $0 | **For now, only LCA admins, club reps, the president and standing directors may set an entry fee above $0** (settled by K, Mon, Oct 19, 2026). Anyone else who tries is told, in words they can follow, that LCA must first set up an account for them inside LCA's own Stripe account, which Stripe calls a connected account (Stripe Connect), so their money is kept apart from LCA's and reaches them. Standing directors may set fees and are told the same thing about reaching their money; until a connected account exists for one, the fees are paid into LCA's account and LCA passes them on (K took the default on Mon, Oct 19, 2026). Regional representatives are not on the list. Who pays Stripe's fee on a connected account, and who handles refunds and disputes, is Q3. |
| D25 | The switch for member-created tournaments | **An LCA admin turns it on and off** (settled by K, Tue, Oct 20, 2026: "this public access to tournament creation, etc should be a switch i can turn on and off as an lca admin"). It is a site setting in Admin, not a build switch: it needs no deploy, it stays off until K has tested, and turning it off stops new creation only. Events already created keep running, and their organizers and entrants see a short plain note with a direct way to reach the LCA admins. |
| D26 | Member-created tournaments | **Any active LCA member may create one; it is never listed and is shared by link** (settled by K, Tue, Oct 20, 2026: "Who? Any active LCA member"). It is never on the tournaments pages, the home page, the news digest, the monthly results, the champions archive, the sitemap or the structured data, and its pages tell search engines not to list them (noindex). It has every other function: registration, pairings, standings, event day, the scanner, the results page and the rating report. At creation the organizer ticks one eligibility box, 18 or older and responsible for an event LCA does not run; no birthdate is asked. The organizer may require LCA membership of entrants. An entry fee above $0 needs the organizer's connected account (D24); until it exists the organizer is told so in plain words, with a quick way to reach the LCA admins, and is not left stuck. The rating report carries only the affiliate ID and director IDs the organizer enters as their own: the site refuses LCA's affiliate ID and never fills in LCA's director IDs, so US Chess has nothing to bill LCA for. At most three open events per organizer; every page carries "Report this event", which lands in the admin queue; an admin can close an event or remove a member's ability to create; admins see every member-created event and how often the service is used and by whom, and representatives do not. Adopting a member's event into the listed set is left for later. What counts as an active member is Q5. |
| D27 | Not an LCA event | **Every member-created event says so** (settled by K, Tue, Oct 20, 2026: "it is also necessary for the people getting those links to participate in their tournaments that they are not officially run and rated LCA events"). The event page, the checkout, every email and the printed kit say "Organized by <name>. Not an LCA event." Emails go out with the organizer as the reply-to. The registration form tells entrants that the event is not run or rated by LCA, and that their name, US Chess ID and email go to the organizer (for a child, the guardian's email). Under-18s show as first name and last initial on any pre-event list (D15). |
| D28 | The scoresheet scanner | **For active LCA members only** (settled by K, Tue, Oct 20, 2026: "Make this how the scanner works too, only active LCA memberships"), in place of today's gate of any signed-in account (`requireAuthedMember` in `functions/api/scan/index.ts`); what counts as active is Q5. The gate takes effect with WS15's `scanner2` (Phase 5); until then today's scanner keeps today's gate (Q39). With K's page 16 picks of Mon, Oct 19, 2026: a scan saves itself at once as a private draft, and its photo is kept 30 days beside the game, in place of today's promise that photos are never stored; there is no Public share, nobody can open a person's profile and see their games, and there is no joined list of games; a game can be linked to any tournament game its player played or is playing, and that link is its public side, encouraged and never forced (K: "i like how we encourage but dont force that"). |
| D29 | A club's contact | **Relayed, never shown** (settled by K, Mon, Oct 19, 2026: "contact emails are to never be shared, this is how we maintain trust with the volunteers"). The club page's contact form sends to the club's own email when the club has given one (clubs are encouraged to, which lightens the representative's load), and otherwise to the region's representative, whose copy says the club has no connected email yet. The messages are relayed to the club and not stored in LCA's database the way support tickets are; LCA only makes sure the connection exists. |
| D30 | A walkthrough of everything built | **For the very end** (asked by K, Wed, Oct 21, 2026: "a way to view all build aspects of the site like a demo or a LCA board walkthrough ... This does not need to be planned upon or built out yet. just remember for last."). Nothing is planned or built for it before the last workstream has landed (section 4). The phase 2 replan's section 8 reads it as a guided walkthrough for the LCA board, every page and every role's screens with sample data, reachable from one place; that reading is not a decision. |

**Open questions carried from the phase 1 replan** (`DESIGN_REPLAN_phase1.md` sections 3, 7.7 and 9, plus two wordings this brief proposes for the replan's section 11 decisions, S11.1 and S11.2). K answered three, and the brief applies them: S7.1 (Register my family shows only signed out or with an active Family membership; everyone else's Register button lists their children in the same picker; the co-guardian case is S7.1b below), S7.11 (round alerts are email only until SMS is built, consistent with D8) and S9.1 (Who's coming keeps A's rating-sorted table with section chips on the event page while the page 4 preview groups by section). The rest are not decided in this brief. Each default below is the working assumption, marked "assumed, K to confirm", and the text above follows it until K says otherwise.

| Ref | Question | Working assumption |
|---|---|---|
| S3.1 | Phone calendar: keep Calendar A (the month grid over the day's agenda), or B or C? | Keep Calendar A (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S3.2 | CSV export for directors: moves to the TD tools (WS08), or returns as a table-footer link for `requireTournamentView` only? | Moves to the TD tools, where the entrants list already lives (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S3.3 | Map defaults: does "Update the list as I move the map" start ticked, and where does the map open? | Starts ticked; opens on the Gulf South; zooms to Louisiana when a Louisiana region is picked (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S3.4 | Saved table views for signed-in users: drop them from WS04? | Dropped; the URL holds every view and a weekly saved search covers the recurring case (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S3.5 | The `.ics` Subscribe link for the current filters: where does it go? | At the end of the info note ("Subscribe to this list") on every view (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S3.6 | The not-yet-open LCA preview leads with "Remind me when it opens" and gains Register when registration opens, departing from the Register-first order. | Keep (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S7.1b | Does a co-guardian whose child is covered by another guardian's Family plan see Register my family? | Yes (replan 7.3); K's quote covers only the viewer's own membership (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S7.2 | Is 13 the right age for a child's own login, and are the 13-or-older checkbox plus the recorded consent checkbox enough? | Yes: 13 or older with both checkboxes recorded; under 13, no login and no child email (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S7.3 | After a teen gets her own login, can guardians and the teen turn off copies? | Each guardian can turn off their own copies; the teen cannot turn guardians' copies off before she manages her own profile (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S7.4 | Should a self-declared "I'm 18 or older" take effect at once? | After 7 days; guardians are emailed at once and any guardian can cancel; a second request needs the owner's approval (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S7.5 | Is moving history from a duplicate profile acceptable without an admin when the same-person check passes? | Yes, with every move logged in `profile_moves`; anything failing the check is refused with a plain message, not queued for volunteers (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S7.6 | In a separation, who can remove a co-guardian? | Only the owner; co-guardians can only leave; LCA takes no role in custody disputes beyond following the owner (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S7.7 | In the TD console, does a guardian show as a full name, and can TDs search by a parent's name? | Full name and searchable, inside the TD console only (`requireTournamentManager`), never on public pages (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S7.8 | Can a parent with 4 or more children change which 3 the Family plan covers mid-year? | No; the ticks change at renewal, an empty spot can be filled any time, and paid entries keep their price (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S7.9 | Where does the scholastic emergency contact from `Account-B` live? | Once on the owner's account, reused on each child's scholastic entry and shown only to that event's TD; WS06's emergency contact bullet and `registrations.emergency_contact_json` follow this (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S7.10 | Should a short public LCA member number be added? | No; the US Chess ID, the parent's name in the TD console and the per-profile card QR are enough (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S7.12 | Can the TD membership desk sell a Family plan for cash and turn walk-in guest children into the payer's profiles on the spot? | Yes, because the TD sees the family in person; otherwise through the emailed "Save these details, create an account" link (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S9.2 | Section picker with many sections: above four sections the card's segmented picker becomes a select. | Keep (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S9.3 | A featured festival and a live event on the home page: does Live outrank the featured festival, or does the festival always lead? | Live outranks it, except that when the live or event-day event belongs to the featured festival the festival hero stays and carries the live line (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S9.4 | Round alerts default: registered players opted in at registration with a switch off, or opt-in? | Opted in at registration with a switch off; WS07 carries this default (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S9.5 | Festival bundle price ("Open + Blitz") dropped because pricing cannot express it. | Dropped; no bundle pricing (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S9.6 | The State Championship's business meeting is a side event of kind `meeting` with an RSVP. | Keep; WS14's `/meeting` links to that side event for its RSVP (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S11.1 | Good to know's Membership item when the event has switched the requirement off (WS05). | "No LCA membership needed" (assumed, K to confirm). |
| S11.2 | The picker's Not covered row state under D11 (WS06). | "Not on your Family plan. An LCA membership (Scholastic $5) is added in step 3.", shown only when the event requires a membership (assumed, K to confirm). |

**Open questions carried from the phase 2 replan** (`DESIGN_REPLAN_phase2.md` section 5). K's picks and answers of Thu, Oct 15 to Tue, Oct 20, 2026 answer P2.11, P2.17, P2.19, P2.20, P2.21 and P2.29 and part of P2.18, and P2.23 is settled by D5 and D11. The rest are not decided. Each default below is the working assumption, marked "assumed, K to confirm"; in the workstream text a default reads "(assumed, P2.N)", and the text follows it until K says otherwise.

| Ref | Question | Working assumption |
|---|---|---|
| P2.1 | Is more coming for page 7, and what stays on the alphabetical pairings sheet's small line? | This note is the page 7 feedback, and anything later becomes a second round; the sheet keeps the order line ("Alphabetical by player") and drops the how-to words, with the round start and the "Pts" key on that small line (assumed, K to confirm). |
| P2.2 | Player reports in the first build, as a steer with no opponent confirmation? | Yes, as D6 now reads (assumed, K to confirm). |
| P2.3 | Who may report a result? | The two signed-in players in that game, not a guardian; letting the guardian of a registered child report that child's game (a child under 13 has no login, D13) is the other way (assumed, K to confirm). |
| P2.4 | Are reported results shown publicly before the TD confirms them? | No: public pairings, standings and the TV show only TD-entered results, and the reporter sees "Reported, awaiting TD" (assumed, K to confirm). |
| P2.5 | When both players report the same result, does the TD still confirm it? | Yes, in one tap; nothing posts by itself (assumed, K to confirm). |
| P2.6 | The TD view on the public pairings and standings pages as well as in the TD console? | Both, behind a "TD view · Public view" switch, for the event's TDs and LCA admins (assumed, K to confirm). |
| P2.7 | Which view does the hall TV open on? | One at a time, cycling one section every 15 seconds, until the TD picks; All on one screen is marked "Suggested" at 30 players or fewer and is unavailable above 16 boards, with the reason in words; the screen never switches by itself (assumed, K to confirm). |
| P2.8 | Standings on the TV in All on one screen? | Alternate pairings and standings every 15 seconds once results are confirmed, with "Show standings after results come in" on by default and a switch to turn it off (assumed, K to confirm). |
| P2.9 | Who sees the US Chess rating report in the standings Download menu? | Only the people who manage the event, everyone `requireTournamentManager` admits for it; everyone else sees the PDF, CSV and wall chart; the report is also the console's Rating report step (assumed, K to confirm). |
| P2.10 | How is the rating report proven before a real event? | An automated check of the export against US Chess's published file layout, then K's own test upload of a finished event; the Phase 2 pilot waits until that test imports cleanly (assumed, K to confirm). The fix brief's smaller points are Q8. |
| P2.11 | Which directory option? | Answered: `Clubs-H` and `Clubs-H-2` (K, Thu, Oct 15, 2026). |
| P2.12 | Near me distance: road miles or straight line? | Road miles, worked out once per club and cached, shown as "about N mi" (assumed, K to confirm). |
| P2.13 | Keep "Copy link to this list"? | No: the filters live in the address, and `Clubs-H` draws none (assumed, K to confirm). |
| P2.14 | Keep the home page's this-week strip, which K kept on Thu, Oct 8, 2026 for WS09? | Not built for now, and `homeWeek` stays off. The phase 2 replan's default kept the strip, with a day opening the directory filtered to clubs meeting that day; K has since picked `Clubs-H` and `Club-G`, which have no day filter and compute nothing from a weekly schedule, after writing on Tue, Oct 13, 2026 that the clubs design "should not attempt to leverage accurate weekly information". If K keeps the strip, it needs a schedule model of its own, planned then (assumed, K to confirm). |
| P2.15 | Border clubs: are Appendix C's regions right? | Appendix C as it stands; K corrects any on the admin club page (assumed, K to confirm). |
| P2.16 | Who keeps an open seat's listings? | LCA admins, until the seat is filled (assumed, K to confirm). |
| P2.17 | Which club page option? | Answered: `Club-G` and `Club-G-2`, with the bare state `Club-Final-Bare` (K, Thu, Oct 15 and Mon, Oct 19, 2026). |
| P2.18 | Retire `club_rep`, `members.club_id` and the admin page's "club members" designation? | The club rep stays (D17). ---

## Appendix A · Evidence cheat sheet (sources for the "why" lines)
- **Hidden navigation.** Desktop hamburger menus were used 27% of the time against ~50% for visible or combo nav. They were ≥39% slower and cut discoverability by >20%. On mobile, combo nav was used 86% of the time against 57% for hidden menus. (NN/g)
- **Audience-based navigation** fails because people approach by task, not job title. (NN/g; GOV.UK)
- **Carousels.** About 1% of homepage visitors click one, and 84% of those clicks land on slide 1 (Notre Dame data). Auto-advance without a pause control fails WCAG 2.2.2 (Level A).
- **List vs grid.** A dated list beat a month grid for finding dates across month breaks. (Hund, Dowell & Mueller 2014)
- **Checkout abandonment** averages ~70%:
  - Extra costs cause 40%.
  - Forced accounts cause 18% (24% in 2022).
  - The average checkout has 11.3 fields where 8 would do.
  - 62% of sites don't make guest checkout prominent.
  - 19% of people abandon over card-form distrust. (Baymard)
- **Map beside list.** 95% used the map when it sat beside the list; 65% never found it on list-only sites. (Baymard)
- **Donations.** Only 4% of nonprofit sites said where donations go. (NN/g)
- **Contrast.**
  - Gold `#c8a94a` on white: 2.28:1.
  - Gold on navy `#1a2744`: 6.51:1.
  - Dark gold `#866a1e` on white: 5.13:1, and 4.7:1 on `#f5f5f2`.
  - White on live red `#ff4757`: 3.34:1, so use dark text on it.
- **Peer patterns:**
  - NC Chess: internal pages for clearinghouse events, a club freshness loop, clickable stats.
  - KingRegistration: ID-first entry and expiry flags.
  - Chess67: QR posters (the Campbell story); its link previews show "Loading…".
  - Zextaos (zextaos.com): added by K on Thursday, October 22 as a peer to review beside Chess67 when the site is being optimised, later in the project; LCA runs no scholastic programs of that kind today, so this is for a future look, not for any workstream now.
  - parkrun: club "when + first visit".
  - Luma: count line.
  - Councilmatic and Keep a Changelog: governance history.
  - USA Pickleball, AMNH and Patreon: membership cards.
  - Wikimedia and GiveDirectly: donation framing.
- **Full source list:** the canvas board `Sources` (~236 entries) and `Start-Audit` (current-site findings with file names).

- **Membership:** `Member-E` and `Member-E-2` (picked), with `Member-F`'s "What your dues do" section in place of Member-E's "What your dues fund" (redraw pending); `Give-A`, the plain Give page (not named by K; assumed, Q6); `Member-D`, the digital card. For comparison: `Member-F`…`Member-J` (each with `-2`) and round 1's `Member-A`…`Member-C`.
- **Account (My LCA):** the hub, `Account-Final-Hub` and `Account-Final-Hub-2` (decided). Picked: `Account-D` and `Account-D-2` (the default page; redraw on phones pending), `Account-H` (Today; redraw with its empty state pending), and `Account-I`, `Account-L`, `Account-N`, `Account-P`, `Account-R` and `Account-S`, each with its `-2`. For comparison: `Account-Int-A`, `Account-Int-B` and `Account-Int-C` (each with `-2`), `Account-E`, `Account-F`, `Account-G`, `Account-J`, `Account-K`, `Account-M`, `Account-O` and `Account-Q` (with their part 2s where drawn), and round 1's `Account-A`…`Account-C`.
- **Work:** picked, each with its `-2`: `Work-E` (TD console), `Work-H` (My region), `Work-J` (Admin) and `Work-L` (setup checklist; redraw without the jump list pending). For comparison: `Work-F`, `Work-G`, `Work-I` and `Work-K` (each with `-2`), and round 1's `Work-A`…`Work-D`.

Every option board carries a grey note with What / Borrowed from / Trade-off. The decided boards for pages 1 to 5 live in `docs/redesign/decided-boards`; the boards for pages 7 to 16 are in `docs/redesign/canvas/boards`, all but `Member-D`, which is opened on the canvas, with each round's record in `docs/redesign/rounds/` (the spec each board was drawn from, and K's words). The decided boards carry a title strip ("Built from", "Replaces", "K's feedback applied"), a numbered legend and a "K's feedback, and where it landed" checklist (items 4.1 to 4.7 on page 4). Invented details on the boards are badged "Sample data" (on the decided boards: entry and section counts, which events register on LCA, type counts, entrant names; on pages 7 to 16 also players, ratings, results, representative names, club colours, logos, distances and post text); everything else is real LCA data, with event names, dates, cities and organizers from the Gulf South feed.

**Where a board and this brief differ, the brief wins:**
- `Live-Final-Pairings` note 4 says the page checks every 60 seconds; D7's 20 seconds stands.
- `Live-Final-TV` frame A's "This is the default above 30 players" is the suggestion only: the stored default is One at a time until the TD picks (P2.7).
- `Live-Final-Print` note 3 keeps "find your name, then go to your board" under the order line; those words are dropped (P2.1).
- `Schol-Final` and `Schol-Final-2` head Schol-A's guide "Before your first event" and leave Schol-A's questions out; until they are redrawn, WS11's text wins: the guide is "Your child's first tournament", and the questions sit under "Before your first event".
- `About-Final`'s New Orleans Metro row says "... and 3 youth programs"; all eight are clubs, and LCA has no youth programs.
- `Account-N` shows the Lafayette Open 2026 as "Published" with "31 registered"; it is a partner event, which never gets a count or a Register button and says "Registers on the organizer's site ↗".
- `Account-Final-Hub-2` section G, rule 1, draws LCA admins and regional representatives drafting an event and naming its one-event director now (Mon, Oct 19, 2026); until K answers Q1, the representatives create from WS09's My region in Phase 3.

## Appendix C · Club regions (D10)

All 25 clubs are assigned by geography to the seven official regions in `src/lib/regions.ts`. K corrects any that are off. To apply, a builder runs `club_regions.sql` (it ships beside this brief) against the local database only (`--local`). Production gets the same assignments from WS09's numbered migration, `00xx_club_regions.sql`, which the merge job applies (0.2) and which sets a club's region only where it is blank, so a correction K has made stands. Afterward K runs `SELECT id, name, region FROM clubs WHERE region IS NULL OR region = ''` on production, a read (0.2), which must return no rows; any row it does return is a club whose stored name differs from the list below.

| Region | Clubs |
|---|---|
| New Orleans Metro | Downriver Chess Club · Greater New Orleans Chess Club · New Orleans Westbank Chess Club (Gretna) · Marrero Chess Organization · North Kenner Library Chess Club · Metairie Chess Academy · Knight Light Chess · Strategic Thoughts NOLA |
| North of Lake Pontchartrain | Mandeville Chess Club · Slidell Chess Club · Picayune Chess Club (Picayune, MS; the nearest region for this border club) |
| South Central Louisiana | Baton Rouge Chess Club · Bluebonnet Chess Club (Baton Rouge) · Gonzales Chess Club · Lafayette Chess Club · Heart of Worship Church Chess (Opelousas) |
| Bayou Region | Houma Chess Club · Morgan City Chess Club |
| Southwest Louisiana | Casa de Ajedrez (Sulphur) · Beauregard Parish Youth Chess (DeRidder) |
| Central Louisiana | Bunkie Chess Club · Pineville Homeschool Chess Club |
| North Louisiana | Shreveport-Bossier Chess Club · Monroe Chess Club · Ruston Knights Chess Club |

Judgment calls worth a second look:
- **Lafayette and Opelousas** sit in Acadiana. They're in South Central rather than Southwest, which is kept for the Lake Charles side.
- **Morgan City** (St. Mary Parish) is in the Bayou Region with Houma rather than in South Central.
- **Picayune** is in Mississippi, about 20 miles from Slidell.
- **The "Baton Rouge / East Central Representative" board seat** wasn't matched to a region by migration 0050's seat seeding. Map it to South Central Louisiana in the Board seats admin so the Baton Rouge clubs have a rep.
